[SUMMARY]
>>>
Spec interview for flightcrew-core, the second attempt at the flightcrew orchestration system, driven by ten registered problems from the v1 problem documents. The v2 draft was abandoned and a clean v1 written at flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/spec.v1.json, rewritten twice: first as a build plan of 98 behaviours, then at outcome altitude (35 behaviours) after the spec-description and verification-addendum manuals were read. Settled: launch/<name>/{specs/,runs/run-<n>/} layout with launch.json as an index and no phase or gate state; configuration under flightdeck/.controlcenter/ (flightcrew.yaml, launch.yaml); freeze in the document; liftoffs as JSON recipes in flightdeck/flightcrew/liftoffs/ driving a workflow suite (fc-plan-<kind>, fc-contracts, fc-build calling fc-verify and fc-review, fc-report); a steward role runs scripts for workflows; a stalled worker gets one stronger attempt then the run stops; the ending after the report (PR merge, branches, diagnosis) is the human's; the flight dispatcher is deferred and core scripts run by path; the spec chain, spec schema and rubric are byte-frozen; the rubric was corrected to v2.4 and moved to flightdeck/flightcrew/checks/rubrics/.
Nine judge passes and one attack (44 findings) were absorbed and ledgered under specs/interview/; the draft was frozen at 852b77c then reopened as draft after the altitude rewrite, so the freeze commit on the branch is void. Remaining: commit the reopened draft, then open a separate characterization launch whose product is a test suite at the human-observable boundary (labelled kept-deliberately or may-change), run it under the v1 tooling as the first real run, and cut flightcrew-core to the deltas (fc ripped out, workflows as conductor, launch/run layout, liftoffs, added roles, three verdicts) against that suite.
Lessons recorded: interview by asking the provider how they would solve each problem, never invent mechanisms (run.json, fc-end, HALT.json, gates were all invented and removed); answer questions before acting; behaviours are outcomes with their reason, not dispatch mechanics; an interface is a common language or location shared by several actors; constraints are what existed before the system.

Yes — a handful, all of them things that change what I do first. I've read the draft, the register, the ledger, the run log, and the approved page; I have not opened the seven problem dumps yet (they come next, before the intent wave).

**1. Where this spec lives.** My dispatch contract names `flightdeck/launch/specs/<spec-name>/` as the canonical folder, but the draft and its `interview/` sit at `flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/` — which is exactly the layout `P-interfaces-launch-layout` proposes (`launch/<name>/specs/` + `runs/run-<n>/`). I'll treat the file where it is as canonical and never move it, and leave the `$schema` string untouched per SC9. Confirm.

**2. Reduction shows up as `retired`, not as deletion.** v1 was frozen at `5f69a94`, so the versioning rule binds: every removed node moves to `retired` with `at` and a note, every id ever used stays on the page. "Fewer nodes" will therefore mean fewer *live* nodes; the retired list will be long. When you say ~60 max, I read that as live nodes across SC + C + I + B + E + D. Confirm that count, or tell me a different one.

**3. Intent is already interviewed.** Seven intent bundles are `answered`, three round pages exist, and the v2 intent node is already `changed` to their result. Two ways to "start with intentions": (a) one confirmation round — I hold the current intent text against the ten problems and ask only where a problem pulls the sentence (e.g. does "state shaken out as open documents" now commit to *JSON documents with schemas*; does "a crew that builds from a spec" survive the runner's demotion); or (b) reopen intent from scratch. I recommend (a). Which?

**4. Prior answers as inputs.** Should I re-read the seven answered intent bundles as settled facts for this session (they're mine under the spec folder, so I may), or treat only the intent node text as what survived?

**5. The surface.** I'll render each round to `interview/pages/round-<n>.html` from the approved page (one `.bundle` per problem, marker, ≤5 questions, ★ recommendation, comments, Copy-answers block), continuing at round-4, and you paste the copied block back in chat. Do you want me to open each page in Chrome for you as well, or just write the file and give you the path?

**6. "Merged assets that don't need remaking".** I'll establish what's built and acceptable through explorers against the working tree at HEAD on `flightcrew-buildout`. Is that the right reference, and do the two launch folders (`flightcrew-buildout`, `flightcrew-buildout-2`) count as record only, never as something the run reproduces?

**7. Extra problems.** After reading the dumps I expect one or two candidates the ten don't cover (my early suspicion: the manuals — four are written as command sequences and are bound to move with the seam; and the spec-chain freeze in SC6/D4, which `P-scope-roster-and-separations` reopens). I'll bring any candidate as its own bundle in the intent round, with the option to reject. Fine?

**8. `agent-shaped`.** The product is a crew of agents, hooks and role files; the intent bundle `P-intent-agent-shaped` was asked. If the answer was yes, every behaviour opens with a check-class tag — which matters for your "behaviours first, then find the check" rule, since `[judged]` and `[statistical]` are exactly the classes v1 dodged. I'll confirm from the bundle unless you tell me now.

Nothing else blocks. Answer 1–5 and I start the wave; 6–8 I can carry into the first round.

Command: mkdir -p /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-dylangraham-Projects-flightdeck/56570eaf-ec3e-4c58-ad0c-c14d3c5be090/scratchpad && cat > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-dylangraham-Projects-flightdeck/56570eaf-ec3e-4c58-ad0c-c14d3c5be090/scratchpad/render.py <<'EOF'
import json,sys,html,glob,os
# usage: render.py <round-n> "<title>" "<lead>" marker1 marker2 ...
ip='~/Projects/flightdeck/flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/interview/'
n,title,lead,*markers=sys.argv[1:]
esc=html.escape
reg={r['id']:r for r in json.load(open(ip+'problems.json'))}
parts=[]
for m in markers:
    b=json.load(open(ip+f'bundles/{m}.json'))
    kind=reg.get(m,{}).get('kind','gap')
    parts.append(f'<div class="bundle" data-marker="{m}">\n<h2>bundle <code>{m}</code> · {kind} · {b["domain"]}</h2>\n<p class="problem">{esc(b["problem"])}</p>')
    for q in b['questions']:
        multi=q.get('multi',False)
        typ='checkbox' if multi else 'radio'
        parts.append(f'<div class="q" data-id="{q["id"]}">\n  <h3><span>{q["id"]}</span>{esc(q["text"])}{" <em style=color:var(--dim);font-size:12px>(pick any)</em>" if multi else ""}</h3>\n  <p class="why">{esc(q["why"])}</p>')
        for o in q['options']:
            rec=' <span class="rec">★</span>' if o.get('recommended') else ''
            parts.append(f'  <label class="opt"><input type="{typ}" name="{q["id"]}" value="{esc(o["value"])}"> {esc(o["value"])}{rec}</label>')
        parts.append(f'  <textarea name="{q["id"]}_c" placeholder="Comment"></textarea>\n</div>')
    parts.append(f'<textarea name="{m}_bc" placeholder="Bundle comment"></textarea>\n</div>')
tpl=open('~/Projects/flightdeck/dev/workspace/filebox/spec-interviewer-interview.html').read()
head=tpl[:tpl.index('<main>')+len('<main>')]
tail=tpl[tpl.index('<section><h2'):]
head=head.replace('<title>Spec interview · agent-spec-interviewer · round 3 — three problems</title>',f'<title>flightcrew-core v2 · round {n} — {esc(title)}</title>')
head=head.replace('<h1>agent-spec-interviewer · round 3 — three bundles, one problem each</h1>',f'<h1>flightcrew-core v2 · round {n} — {esc(title)}</h1>')
open(ip+f'pages/round-{n}.html','w').write(head+f'\n<p class="lead">{lead}</p>\n'+"\n".join(parts)+"\n\n"+tail)
print('rendered',ip+f'pages/round-{n}.html')
EOF
echo ok

Command: python3 - <<'EOF'
import json
sp='flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-core/specs/spec.v2.json'
s=json.load(open(sp))
def node(id,text,status='new',note=None,**kw):
    d={"id":id,"status":status}; d.update(kw); d["text"]=text
    if note: d["note"]=note
    return d
def retire(section,id,note):
    for n in s[section]:
        if n['id']==id:
            s['retired'].append({"id":id,"at":2,"text":n['text'],"note":note})
            s[section]=[m for m in s[section] if m['id']!=id]; return
    raise SystemExit('missing '+id)

# ---- scope
s['scope']=[
 node('SC1',"flightdeck/flightcrew/: the flight dispatcher and the leaf scripts under bin/, each leaf runnable by its own path; the hook scripts and the settings fragment; one crew file per role; the schemas; the templates; the workflow scripts; and the shared libraries the leaves, hooks and validators import.",'changed',"v1 named the fc runner, its command modules and MANIFEST.txt; v2 names the flight dispatcher, leaves that stand alone, and drops the manifest from scope.",kind='in'),
 node('SC2',"flightdeck/launch/: the launch README, FLIGHTLOG.md, and one folder per launch at launch/<name>/ holding specs/ (the spec series, the tests-map series and interview/) and runs/run-<n>/ (one folder per attempt); launch/flightcrew-core/ is this work's own launch and its first run is produced by the system it builds.",'changed',"v1 placed specs at launch/specs/<S>/ and one folder per attempt named as a launch; v2 adopts launch/<name>/{specs/,runs/run-<n>/} and FLIGHTLOG.md.",kind='in'),
 node('SC3',"flightdeck/testbench/: fixtures, lib and benches, and only those suites whose cases cover a leaf, hook, validator or crew rule this spec keeps; every suite whose subject this version retires is deleted in this run; a run's own checks live in that run's folder, not here.",'changed',"v1 held one suite per T id; v2 keeps the testbench as shared fixtures and a regression floor for kept assets and moves a run's checks into the run.",kind='in'),
 node('SC4',"flightdeck/manuals/: launch-anatomy, journey, workflows and review rewritten to describe artefacts, roles and gates with no command sequence in them; the testing manuals and library/terms.md aligned to one testing vocabulary; the spec, versioning and rubric manuals unchanged.",'changed',"v1 listed the manual set; v2 names the four command-sequence manuals as rewritten and the testing vocabulary as aligned.",kind='in'),
 node('SC5',"flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout/, flightdeck/launch/flightcrew-buildout-2/ and flightdeck/launch/specs/flightcrew-v1/ are deleted from the branch; git history keeps them and nothing in the new layout reads them.",'new',"The two v1 attempts were not flightcrew runs and their folders carry the old shape.",kind='in'),
 node('SC6',"Out: flightdeck/blackbox/, flightdeck/hangar/, flightdeck/missions/, flightdeck/radar/, library/, dev/ and the dev-workspace skill are never written; .claude/ receives only what the install step names.",'changed',"v1 also forbade reading library/; v2 reads it as the source pattern and still never writes it.",kind='out'),
 node('SC7',"Out: the spec schema, the spec template, the readiness rubric, the rubric bench, the spec and versioning manuals, and the three spec-chain crew files (spec-builder, spec-judge, spec-attacker) are unchanged in any byte.",'changed',"v1 allowed two named edits to spec-builder.md; v2 leaves the whole chain as it stands.",kind='out'),
 node('SC8',"Out: a local web control surface over the run documents, a judge runner that calls a model to grade a judged check, CI configuration or any remote automation, telemetry beyond the events file, and any npm package.",'changed',"v1 excluded the judge runner, an HTML display served by a process and telemetry; v2 adds the control surface and CI by name.",kind='out'),
 node('SC9',"Out: installing anything — no global command, no package.json, no symlink into the home directory; the dispatcher and every leaf are invoked by path.",'changed',"fc becomes flight; the rule is unchanged.",kind='out'),
 node('SC10',"Out: rewriting the $id or $schema strings of any schema, template or spec; editing the repository .gitignore; copying a spec, a tests map or an interview/ folder into a run folder; migrating spec folders on other branches to the new layout.",'changed',"v1 forbade copying interview/ only; v2 forbids every copy, because a run points at the series by version and commit.",kind='out'),
]
# ---- decisions
for id,note in [
 ('D2',"one entry point as the surface a human remembers is the runner-as-control-surface the intent rejects"),
 ('D3',"active-launch discovery by status is replaced by the launch record naming its current run, so guards need no resolution and two active launches cannot occur"),
 ('D13',"a run against a draft spec or map is no longer possible: a run is opened against a frozen series entry and the freeze is read from the document"),
 ('D15',"the orchestrator writing only through three runner commands is replaced by roles writing their own documents and hooks recording"),
 ('D16',"the scribe returns as a role; report assembly by a command that swallows its errors is the failure the problem set records"),
 ('D18',"runs hold no pinned copies; a run points at specs/ beside it by version and commit"),
]: retire('decisions',id,note)
for d in s['decisions']:
    if d['id']=='D4':
        d['status']='changed'; d['text']="The spec chain — spec-builder.md, spec-judge.md, spec-attacker.md — and the spec schema, template, rubric and bench are unchanged in any byte; the spec-builder is a session agent the human starts, and the judge and attacker are subagents it dispatches, which is why their files declare inputs differently from the run-chain roles."; d['note']="v1 froze the chain for calibration and still edited the builder; v2 freezes all three and states why they differ."
    if d['id']=='D5':
        d['status']='changed'; d['text']="Every run document is JSON with a schema and is the source of truth; markdown is rendered only where a human reads it (the dispatch prompts, the report, the log entry) and the plan has no markdown rendering."; d['note']="v1 rendered plan.md and kickoff.md; v2 drops plan.md."
    if d['id']=='D1':
        d['text']="Node ES modules with zero dependencies and no package.json, because the harness must run identically on any machine with Node 22 and git; hooks and leaves are Node too so one test harness covers both."
s['decisions']+= [
 node('D19',"The runner is renamed flight and reduced to a thin dispatcher over leaf scripts, each runnable by its own path, because nothing may depend on the dispatcher and no command a human types advances a run."),
 node('D20',"A node about an artefact's state, a hook's enforcement, a role's inputs and outputs or a rendering's contents belongs in this spec; a node about a command's arguments, resolution order, exit codes or which subcommand does what does not, because the first attempt's tests tested the runner's fidelity to itself."),
 node('D21',"The test-builder is a session agent like the spec-builder: started by the human against a frozen spec and a run folder, able to ask, returning a schema-shaped handoff; it is never dispatched sealed, because it works before anything else exists and needs the human."),
 node('D22',"interface-builder, strong-worker, adversary and scribe are crew roles, not kickoff rules, because each protects a separation the constitution names and a rule without a role has no context isolation."),
 node('D23',"A role's own guarantees are declared as hooks in its frontmatter and global hooks keep only what applies to every role, because Claude Code runs an agent's frontmatter hooks only while that agent acts and a global hook cannot tell roles apart."),
 node('D24',"The launch is the unit of work and the run the unit of attempt: launch/<name>/ holds the spec series and every run, runs are numbered and never reopened, and the log is FLIGHTLOG.md beside all launches, because two attempts at one piece of work must share an address."),
 node('D25',"The v1 attempt folders are deleted rather than migrated, because migrating means editing two frozen maps and two records for evidence that no flightcrew run produced."),
 node('D26',"The flight dispatcher owns exactly two scaffolds, launch init and launch run, because the interval before a spec exists and the start of each attempt were the two moments no tool modelled."),
]
if not s.get('retired'): s['retired']=[]
json.dump(s,open(sp,'w'),indent=2,ensure_ascii=False); open(sp,'a').write('\n')
print(len(s['scope']),len(s['decisions']),len(s['retired']))
EOF

**Summary of key facts:**

1. **SubagentStop &amp; Stop**: Both carry `last_assistant_message`, `agent_id`, `agent_type`. Only Stop also carries `transcript_path` as a common field. SubagentStop includes a `reason` field.

2. **PostToolUse &amp; SessionEnd**: PostToolUse carries `tool_input` (object) and `tool_response` (string | object). SessionEnd carries `reason` (why session ended).

3. **SubagentStart**: Carries `agent_type` (yes) and `agent_id`.

4. **Workflows**: Scripts live in `.claude/workflows/&lt;name&gt;.js` (project) or `~/.claude/workflows/&lt;name&gt;.js` (personal). Invoked as `/&lt;name&gt;`. When `agent(prompt, {schema: {...}})` is called, it returns the validated/parsed JSON object matching that schema. Scripts have **no** file/shell access themselves (agents do). Scripts must end with a `return` statement. `workflow()` nesting does **not** exist. Runs are recorded to `~/.claude/projects/&lt;session_id&gt;/`, and `args` is received pre-parsed as JSON.</result>

**Summary of key findings:**

1. **Sandbox keys**: `sandbox.filesystem.denyRead` and `sandbox.filesystem.denyWrite` can live in project `.claude/settings.json`.

2. **PreToolUse hooks**: Yes, they can deny Bash commands via exit code 2, and they receive `tool_input.command`.

3. **Permissions.deny coverage**: Edit/Write deny rules cover only Bash redirections and recognized file commands (`cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed`), NOT arbitrary subprocess writes. Bash deny patterns (`Bash(pattern)`) are the proper mechanism for denying Bash writes by pattern.

4. **Stop hook override**: After **8 consecutive blocks** without progress, Claude Code overrides the hook. Stderr from exit 0 hooks is **not shown** to the user or model—only stdout (for specific events) is added as plain text to Claude's context.</result>

## Part (b): Lines 80-115 from testing-description.md

```
80	**The exit code** — the deterministic verdict: pass or fail, with no third state. Spoken by the structural, behavioural, artefact, invariant and project-rule classes. Its law is finality: an exit code cannot be argued with, only investigated.
81	
82	**The ratio** — the statistical verdict: successes over trials, read against a threshold declared before any trial ran. Spoken by the statistical class alone. Its law is pre-commitment: a threshold set after the results are known is not a threshold.
83	
84	**The verdict sheet** — the judged verdict: fixed questions, scored answers, mandatory quotations, in a schema a machine validates first. Spoken by the judged class alone. Its law is evidence: a score that cannot point to the line it scored does not count.
85	
86	## The gating ladder
87	
88	The same check can bind work loosely or absolutely; the ladder is the set of grips, each trading more setup for less reliance on anyone remembering.
89	
90	1. **In the prompt** — the check is asked for. It runs if the agent remembers, which under pressure it may not.
91	2. **As a goal** — the check is a standing condition, re-evaluated after every turn until it resolves or the session is stopped for stalling.
92	3. **As a deterministic gate** — the check runs as a script that blocks completion until it passes; a run that blocks repeatedly has a problem the check cannot fix, and that repetition is itself a stall signal worth halting on.
93	4. **By a second opinion** — a fresh context attempts to refute the passing result. This rung sits on the gate, never in place of it, and is where verification hands over to review.
94	
95	The floor for any unattended run is the third rung, applied to the spec's end-to-end proof.
96	
97	## The four placements
98	
99	A check also has an address — who causes it to run — and checks migrate through four of them as they prove their worth.
100	
101	**Standalone** — invoked deliberately, when wanted: right for cross-cutting sweeps that do not apply to every change. **Embedded** — run automatically by the workflow that produces the thing it checks, so the producer cannot forget it. **Chained** — invoked by another check's completion, so a sequence of verifications runs as one contract. **Enforced** — fired by the machinery itself, hooks on every edit and CI on every change, with no author diligence involved at all.
102	
103	The promotion signal is repetition: a check being invoked by hand after every change has earned the next address along.
104	
105	## Order of assembly
106	
107	1. **Structural** — first and enforced, before any task-specific check exists, because nothing else is worth running against an incoherent result.
108	2. **Behavioural** — with the spec's freeze, derived entry by entry from its IDs, the end-to-end proof among them; these are the checks the spec's verification domain names.
109	3. **Project rules · Artefact** — in unison, as the work's outputs and the project's history justify them: the diff boundary and local laws on one side, the frozen references on the other.
110	4. **Invariant** — where enumeration has visibly run out, and wherever a constraint stated a number.
111	5. **Statistical · Judged** — in unison and only when the artefact is agent-shaped, because the judge exists to read what the probes record, and neither means anything alone.
112	
113	As with the spec, the order is a dependency order rather than a procedure: a judged finding routinely sends its author back to sharpen a behavioural check, and a statistical scenario often reveals a missing control the moment it first runs. What the order forbids is the skip upward — judging what was never probed, probing what was never proven coherent, or gating anything on a signal that is not one of the three.
```

## Part (c): Terminology Definitions from terms.md

Lines defining requested terms:

can I ask an honest question. is this build too big? should I be splitting this up. for eg test, fc command, liftoff, etc?

It seems like im trudging through mud and all your questions seem so tedious. "do you want to keep x y z" I don't know. I only have a vision for my app, agents are the experts at filtering through walls of text.

------------------------------------------------------------

[ASSISTANT 💬]
Honest answer: yes, it's too big — and half the mud is mine.

**On size.** The ten problems are ten features. Layout, JSON foundation, runner, kickoff-as-workflow, check rail with three verdicts and a judge, four new roles, sealed dispatch for every role, fail-closed enforcement, endings performed, diagnosis carried forward. Any three of those would be a full launch. A 60-node spec can't hold all ten without each getting three or four nodes, which is thin enough that the test-builder and planner will have to guess — and guessing is what v1 did. There's also the method's own argument: a smaller spec freezes sooner, gets a run, gets a log entry, and that log entry is what improves the next spec. One huge spec delays the first real observation of flightcrew running, which is the thing you actually want.

**On tedium.** You're right that "do you want to keep x, y, z" is not a vision question. Your problem documents already *are* the vision, in detail; the explorers have given me the facts; sorting the two against each other is my job, not yours. I've been putting sorting decisions in front of you to get cover. The viewer is the better contract: I write, you read and disagree where it's wrong. From here I'll only bring a question when it's a genuine fork of vision — the kind where two good systems could go either way and only you know which one you want.

**A split I'd recommend**, in dependency order, each its own launch with its own log entry:

1. **flightcrew-core v2 — the ground.** Layout (`launch/<name>/specs/ + runs/run-<n>/`, `FLIGHTLOG.md`), every run document as JSON with a schema, freeze in the document, hooks that fail closed with the sandbox active, `flight` as leaves (`init`, `run`, `check`, `render`, `merge`, `validate`), the roster with reach in frontmatter, `CLAUDE.md`. Outcome: a run folder can be opened, its documents validated, its frozen things held — nothing conducts yet, but everything a conductor needs exists and holds.
2. **conduct — a run under its own power.** Kickoff becomes a workflow plus JSON arguments, phases as prebuilt workflows ending at gates, sealed dispatch and schema'd returns for every role, the four new roles in play, endings performed (branches, worktrees, ground cleared), the log entry defended and carried into the next run. Outcome: the sentence your intent already says.
3. **the check rail** — three verdicts, the judge, run-local checks, test-builder as a session. Could fold into 1 (documents and folders) and 2 (gates), or stand alone after them.

Under that split, what's already placed in v2 (intent, scope, constraints, decisions) survives almost unchanged; I'd narrow the intent's outcome clause to launch 1 and note the series. Interfaces and behaviours then become tractable — maybe 25 nodes instead of 60 — and I'd write them from the problem docs and the facts, with the viewer as your review.

Your call: split as above (my recommendation), a different cut, or keep one spec and accept it'll be thin. Tell me which and I'll re-shape the draft before touching interfaces.

  n("E6","stop-gate runs in a gated phase with no tests map pinned, or with a map file it cannot read: it exits 2 with the reason on stderr and appends a stop_block event; it never exits 0 in a gated phase without running the phase's checks."),
  n("E7","A guard resolves a run in progress and then fails to parse launch.json, run.json, the pinned map or the plan: it prints a deny decision naming the file; event-log in the same case exits 0 and appends one line to hooks.log."),
  n("E8","Two launches hold a current_run whose outcome is null and FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH is unset: the guards deny every Edit, Write and NotebookEdit naming both launches, flight leaves that need a run exit 1 naming both, and event-log exits 0 writing nothing; with FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=<name> that launch is selected."),
  n("E9","A hook receives stdin that is not a JSON object: it exits 0, prints nothing to stdout, and appends one line to hooks.log when a run resolves."),
  n("E10","A check's command names a binary that does not exist or exceeds 300 seconds: its verdict is error, its stderr_tail names the command and the cause, the remaining checks still run, and flight check exits 2."),

  n("E12","flight install --apply finds a target file whose content differs from what it would write: it lists the conflicting paths, writes nothing, and exits 2 unless --force is passed."),
  n("E13","A folder under flightdeck/launch/ has no launch.json: flight validate, the guards and event-log treat it as not a launch and never read inside it."),
  n("E14","A hook runs with $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR unset or pointing at a directory without flightdeck/launch/: it exits 0 and writes nothing."),
 ],
 "decisions":[
  n("D1","Node ES modules with zero dependencies and no package.json, because the harness must run identically on any machine with Node 22 and git; hooks and leaves are Node too so one test harness covers both."),
  n("D2","The runner is named flight and is a thin dispatcher over leaves that run by their own path, because nothing may depend on the dispatcher and no command a human types advances a run."),
  n("D3","The launch is the unit of work and the run the unit of attempt: launch/<name>/ holds the spec series and every run, runs are numbered and never reopened, and the log is FLIGHTLOG.md beside all launches, because two attempts at one piece of work must share an address."),
  n("D4","The first phase is spec: a launch is opened from an idea before any spec is frozen, so the interval in which a spec is made has a run folder, and flight launch run moves the launch to targets only against a frozen spec."),
  n("D5","plan.json is the source of truth and plan.md is rendered from it, because a hand-edited copy drifts and machine consumers need the structured form."),
  n("D6","Worktree isolation is requested per dispatch by the agent() call or the Agent tool for implementer and strong-worker only, and the settings set worktree.baseRef to head, because a worker branched from main would never see the run branch."),
  n("D7","WorktreeCreate is not hooked because a WorktreeCreate command hook replaces Claude Code's own worktree creation and its stdout is read as the directory path; WorktreeRemove is recorded."),
  n("D8","Hooks run in place from flightdeck/flightcrew/hooks/ through $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR and are never copied, because a copied hook loses its relative imports and a second copy drifts."),
  n("D9","The spec chain and its rubric corpus are unchanged because they carry calibration evidence this launch cannot reproduce; spec-builder is a session agent and spec-judge and spec-attacker are subagents it dispatches, which is why their files declare inputs differently from the run chain."),
  n("D10","The conduct of a run (kickoff as workflow and arguments, phases as workflows, endings performed, the diagnosis carried forward) and the ratio and verdict-sheet verdicts with their judge are deliberately deferred to the second and third launches, because each is a launch of its own and this one is the layer they read and write."),
  n("D11","The two v1 attempt folders and the old spec folder stay as history rather than being migrated, because migrating means editing two frozen maps and two records for evidence no flightcrew run produced."),

This launch lays the ground the rest of flightcrew stands on: a piece of work has one home under flightdeck/launch/<name>/ holding its spec series and its numbered runs; every document a run reads or writes is JSON with a schema, written by the thing that produces it; a frozen document is frozen because its own status says so and a hook reads it; every guard fails closed and every guarded path is held against the shell; a worker cannot finish red; the runner is flight, a thin dispatcher over leaves that stand alone, with two scaffolds that give the interval before a spec exists an address; and the crew roster is complete, each role's reach declared in its own file.   n("B1","[deterministic] flight launch init <name> creates launch/<name>/ holding launch.json (current_run 1, gates pending, runs [ { n: 1 } ]), specs/spec.v1.json copied from flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/spec.template.json with name set, an empty specs/interview/, and runs/run-1/ with run.json, an empty events.jsonl and the folders I1 names; every file it writes validates against its schema."),

  n("B3","[deterministic] frozen-guard, on a PreToolUse for Edit, Write or NotebookEdit whose target is a JSON file with a top-level status of frozen, prints a deny decision naming the file and appends a frozen_denied event when a run resolves; the decision is the same with no launch resolved, with launch.json absent, and inside a worktree."),

  n("B5","[deterministic] return-capture, on SubagentStop for an agent_type that has a return schema, parses the fenced JSON in last_assistant_message, validates it against that schema, writes it to runs/run-<n>/returns/<agent_type>-<id>.json and appends a return event; on a parse or schema failure it exits 2 with the validation errors on stderr so the subagent corrects its return."),
  n("B6","[deterministic] worker-gate, the SubagentStop hook in the frontmatter of implementer, strong-worker and interface-builder, runs the checks of the unit named in the agent's dispatch through the check leaf and exits 2 with the failing ids and their last 20 output lines on stderr when any verdict is fail or error, appending a stop_block event; on green it appends check_run and exits 0; a worker therefore cannot finish red."),
  n("B7","[deterministic] On the eighth consecutive stop_block for one agent_id since its newest check_run, worker-gate writes launch.json.halt with the agent_id and reason and exits 0; while halt is set and not cleared every guard denies every Edit, Write and NotebookEdit with a reason naming the halt, flight launch run refuses, and the SessionStart hook prints a systemMessage naming the halt; only a write of halt.cleared releases it."),
  n("B8","[deterministic] event-log appends exactly one line to runs/run-<n>/events.jsonl per recorded hook event carrying ts, event, launch, run, source hook, session_id, agent_id and agent_type when present, and a detail object limited to the allowed keys; on SessionStart it prints a systemMessage naming the launch, the run and the gates cleared."),
  n("B9","[deterministic] flight check runs every check of the pinned tests map that is not quarantined, serially, each as /bin/sh -c with cwd the launch root and a 300 second limit, writes evidence/<T>.json per check and rebuilds evidence/summary.json carrying the counts, the boundary result (files changed since lock_commit outside the allowed paths) and the locked result (changed files under locked paths), and exits 2 when any verdict is fail or error or either result is non-empty; flight check <T...> runs only the named checks and rebuilds the summary from every evidence file present."),
  n("B10","[deterministic] flight render <role> <target> writes dispatches/<role>-<target>.md assembled per I9 whose first line names the role and target, which contains the text of every spec node in the target's spec_refs and no text of any other B or E node, and which names each check by id and covers with no command text."),
  n("B11","[deterministic] flight merge <unit>, given a green return whose branch exists, the checked-out branch equal to run.json.branch and every depends_on unit already merged, merges the unit branch with --no-ff, runs the unit's checks, commits on green, appends unit_merged and removes the worktree and branch; on a red check or a conflict it aborts the merge, changes nothing and exits 2 naming the ids or paths."),

**Workflow guidance from your two explainers, and how it landed in the spec:**

- A run has no phase field — it's the workflows invoked and the gates cleared. `launch.json` now holds only `current_run`, `gates`, `halt` and the runs list (I2, D5). The v1 `fc launch phase` spine is gone entirely.
- Gate a worker on `SubagentStop` in its own frontmatter, not the session `Stop` hook — a worker cannot finish red, and the eighth block writes a halt a human clears (B6, B7, D6). No global stop-gate.
- The script can't touch disk, so persisting is an agent's or a hook's job — that's round 1's Q1.
- Worktrees branch from HEAD, so render then commit then spawn — the render leaf commits its own output (B10, D14).
- `agent()` with `schema` returns validated JSON; `args` is real JSON; one level of `workflow()` nesting; `budget` is a hard ceiling — so ceilings live in the script and its arguments, never in a record. That's why this launch has no ceilings object; the conduct launch carries them as workflow arguments.
- The leaves that survive are exactly the ones the explainer names: render, check (with boundary and locked folded in), merge, validate, plus the two scaffolds and install (I5).

Paste round 1 and any viewer disagreements when you have them; I'll fold the judge's sheet in at the same time, then attack.

------------------------------------------------------------

[ASSISTANT 💬]
All placed. The two corrections are structural and I've applied them across the draft rather than patching one node:

- **No gates, no state file.** `launch.json` is now identity only (`{ schema_version, name, created }`) — the marker that says "this folder is a launch". The latest run is the highest-numbered `runs/run-<n>/`. `run.json` is gone; what it carried moves to `kickoff.json` (pins, branch, base commit, `changed_from_previous`). A halt is a file `runs/run-<n>/HALT.json` the gate writes and the human deletes. The outcome is the `outcome:` line of the log entry, human-written, and `fc-end` reads it from there.
- **Two human readings, then everything human.** Workflows: `fc-plan` (you read the plan) → `fc-contracts` (you read the seams) → `fc-run` (waves, verify, review, fix, scribe report and log stub — no human inside) → you write the outcome → `fc-end`. The orchestrator session invokes each on your word and never the next without it.

On P9, the sources you asked for: the endings guide's abandonment step 5 says "Delete the run's branches, remove its worktrees, prune… a failed unit is a discarded directory" (`endings-guide.html:159–160`), while the map page says on exit "the branch is left as it is" (quoted in structure-problems P4). The endings guide is the one written specifically for endings and carries the reason (the haunted repository); the map page is a summary. I've set the default to **keep** as you answered, with evidence committed on the branch first — the kickoff argument `ending.abandoned_branches` lets a run flip it.

------------------------------------------------------------

[ASSISTANT 💬]
The judge returned five findings, all shape: interfaces missing formats other nodes rely on, compound behaviours, a stubbed end-to-end proof, and a boundary that contains its own exclusions. The last one needs a real fix, not wording — the spec-chain files, spec schema and spec template sit inside `crew/**`, `schemas/**`, `templates/**`. I'm moving this run's assets into subfolders (`crew/run/`, `schemas/run/`, `templates/dispatch/`) so the untouched files sit outside the boundary. What you've just told me about the ending, read back so you can correct it before I write a word of it:

- The build workflow runs through waves, verify and review. If green and the review passes, the workflow ends by opening a PR and informing you. Worktree cleanup is the workflow's.

- Happy: you merge. Not happy: you fill in the run log and try again; you reject the PR and delete the branch.

From that stretch of the conversation, decided and not decided:

**Decided.** `.controlcenter` is git-ignored: "mutable state outside git… nothing gated should depend on it" (5426, restated 5530). `launch.json` is an index that "points at the current spec version, the current run and the hook configuration" and "can hold what spans runs: the spec series, the map series, which run is current, the hook settings, the ceilings" (5420, 5422). Ceilings are since superseded by your point 3 — runtime and agent files.

**Not decided there.** Who writes `.controlcenter` and when; whether it holds anything beyond `LAUNCH_DIRECTORY`; whether a worktree inherits the repository's file or carries its own. Nothing in that range on committing before a worktree.

Three questions, then I'll propose the rewrite of the layout and foundation nodes for you to approve before I touch the file:

1. **`.controlcenter` writer.** Who sets `LAUNCH_DIRECTORY` — you by hand when you start on a launch, or `flight launch run` when it opens a run (and you by hand any other time)?

2. **Current run.** The conversation had `launch.json` pointing at the current run; earlier today you said "neither launch.json holds the latest run, this can be read." Which do you want — `launch.json` names it, or the highest `runs/run-<n>/` is it and nothing names it?

3. **Worktrees.** Workers' hooks need to know the run. Inherit — hooks resolve `.controlcenter` from the main checkout (git's common dir), so one file serves every worktree — or each worktree carries its own copy written when it's created? My reading of "nothing gated may depend on it" favours inherit, since the worker's dispatch already names its run and unit.

## **File Contents with Line Numbers**

### base.md (version 3)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 3 --&gt;
2	## Conduct
3	1. Run `fc launch status`. Read the spec and the tests map at the paths in the header, and the run log named under read first. Dispatch explorer agents for every fact the plan needs that is not stated in the spec, the tests map or the run log at the header paths, one question each. Write each returned object to a file under `returns/` in the launch folder named `explore-X&lt;n&gt;.json`, then store it with `fc return explorer &lt;file&gt; --id X&lt;n&gt;`.
4	2. Dispatch the planner with the spec, the tests map, the run log, the roster and the explorer answers, and take the plan content it returns. Write that content to a file and pass it to `fc plan write &lt;json-path|--stdin&gt;`: it validates the plan, stores plan.json and renders plan.md. HALT: Gate 1. A human reads plan.md against the spec and records `fc launch gate G1 approve` or `fc launch gate G1 exit`. Dispatch nothing until the gate is recorded.
5	3. Wave 0, contracts: `fc worker render &lt;unit&gt;` for the contracts unit and dispatch one implementer with that prompt; store its return with `fc return worker &lt;file&gt; --unit &lt;id&gt;` and land it with `fc worker merge &lt;unit&gt;`. Where the plan carries `no_contracts` instead, wave 0 holds no unit: dispatch nothing, and go straight to the gate. The stop-gate hook runs that unit's checks and the boundary in this phase without being invoked, or the acceptance check where the plan carries `no_contracts`. HALT: Gate 2. A human reads the changed-since-lock list and the wave 0 check results and records `fc launch gate G2 approve` or `fc launch gate G2 exit`.
6	4. Waves 1..n: `fc worker render &lt;unit&gt;` per unit and dispatch one implementer per unit — pilot units first, then the rest in chunks no larger than the implementers_concurrent ceiling, the next chunk starting only when every return of the current one is stored. Store each return with `fc return worker`, land each unit with `fc worker merge &lt;unit&gt;` in wave order, and stop dispatching on any halt return.
7	5. Verify: `fc launch phase verify`, then `fc verify` (checks, boundary, locked paths, budget). When the plan asks for one, `fc verifier render` and dispatch the verifier, then write the verdict to a file under `returns/` named `verify-&lt;n&gt;.json` and store it with `fc return verifier &lt;file&gt; --pass &lt;n&gt;`.
8	6. Review: `fc launch phase review`, then `fc critic render --pass &lt;n&gt;` and dispatch the critic with the sealed prompt. Write the findings to a file under `review/` in the launch folder named `pass-&lt;n&gt;.json` and store them with `fc return critic &lt;file&gt; --pass &lt;n&gt;`. Route a correctness gap or a scope violation back to the implementer that owns the unit whose paths hold the file, re-run `fc verify`, then take a fresh critic pass; a spec conflict is an escalation; an observation is recorded and never dispatched. Record a resolution with `fc return critic --resolve F&lt;n&gt; --commit &lt;sha&gt;`.
9	7. Report: `fc launch phase report`. HALT: Gate 3. A human reads the ledger, the open findings, the unverified lines and the cost, and ends the run with `fc launch end &lt;accepted|accepted-with-reservations|abandoned|partial&gt;` (`partial` also takes `--units`), which renders report.md and the evidence page and inserts the run-log stub.
10	
11	## Escalate
12	Stop and ask a human when the spec is silent or contradictory on something a unit needs, when an implementer reports that a locked check is wrong or unsatisfiable, when an abandon trigger in the plan fires, when a permission or a tool blocks a required action, or when a ceiling is exceeded. Run `fc launch escalate &lt;kind&gt; --detail "…"` naming the finding, where `&lt;kind&gt;` is one of `spec-gap` (the spec is silent or contradictory), `wrong-check` (a locked check is wrong or unsatisfiable), `trigger` (an abandon trigger fired), `blocked` (a permission or tool blocked a required action), `budget` (a ceiling was exceeded) and `halt` (a worker returned a halt), then end the turn; propose nothing else in the same message.
13	
14	## Roles
15	`explorer` receives one question, its stage and the paths it may read, and returns a cited answer; it never receives the plan or a unit's work. `planner` receives the spec, the tests map, the run log, the roster at flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/README.md and the explorer answers, and returns plan content; it never receives worker transcripts. `implementer` receives one unit's rendered dispatch — that unit's spec nodes, its checks by id, its paths and its branch — and returns a worker return; it never receives the plan, the kickoff or another unit's dispatch. `verifier` receives the evidence, the tests map and the merged branch, and returns a verdict; it never receives implementer reasoning. `critic` receives the pinned spec, the diff since the lock and the evidence, and returns findings; it never receives the plan, the kickoff, the returns or earlier findings. Dispatch caps are defaults the plan may tighten and may not loosen: one question per explorer, one unit per implementer, and at most implementers_concurrent implementers at a time. Workers never message each other. Every dispatch is a rendered prompt, with one exception: a review-phase fix dispatch, which the conductor assembles from the finding, the spec node and that unit's checks, and nothing else.
16	
17	## Communication
18	Summaries travel up, transcripts never do. Every return arrives in its declared shape; the conductor writes it to its file under the launch folder — returns/ for worker, explorer and verifier returns, review/ for critic passes — and stores it through fc return; raw check output lives in the evidence, never paraphrased into prose. State what is checked, what is reviewed and what is merely stated, and keep the three apart. The evidence page at the path in the header is rewritten at every check, gate, phase change, return and ending.
19	
20	## Budgets
21	The ceilings live in launch.json and the plan states its expected cost inside them: agents, concurrent implementers, turns per agent, gate iterations, stop blocks, critic passes, minutes, and tokens when they are observed. Defaults hold unless the plan tightens them. Exceeding a ceiling is an escalation.
22	
23	## Outputs
24	One branch per unit named `&lt;launch&gt;/&lt;unit name&gt;`, built in its own worktree and landed by `fc worker merge`. Every agent return stored through `fc return` at its fixed path. The evidence page current at the end of every phase. report.md rendered by `fc launch end`, with the run-log stub inserted and its mechanical fields filled, and the worktree and branch cleanup lines printed for the human to run.
25	
26	## Never
27	Edit a locked path (denied by the lock-guard hook and detected by `fc locked`) · write outside the allowed paths and this launch's folder (denied by the boundary-guard hook and detected by `fc boundary`) · continue after an abandon trigger has fired (the guards deny every edit and `fc` refuses to dispatch) · resolve a spec conflict without a human (`fc launch escalate` is the only route) · hand a worker anything beyond its rendered dispatch (`fc worker render` for a unit; for a review-phase fix, the finding, the spec node and that unit's checks, as step 6 routes it) · hand-edit kickoff.md or plan.md (both are re-rendered from their sources).
```

### README.md
```
1	# Kickoff library
2	
3	The parts under this directory assemble into one launch's `kickoff.md`, the standing instructions the orchestrator of that launch conducts the run from. Nothing else reads them: the spec says what must be true, the plan says how this run is cut, the project constitution — the repository's `CLAUDE.md`, into which `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/constitution-fragment.md` is merged — says how the project always works, and the kickoff says only how to conduct a run of this shape.
4	
5	## Assembling
6	
7	`fc` is `flightdeck/flightcrew/bin/fc`, invoked by path from the repository root; the short `fc &lt;sub&gt;` form below means that binary. `fc launch new` and `fc launch kickoff` render `flightdeck/launch/&lt;L&gt;/kickoff.md`, where `&lt;L&gt;` is the launch name, as a header block followed by `base.md`, one `shape-*.md` and one `task-*.md`, in that order. The parts are named on the command line as `--kickoff base+shape-&lt;s&gt;+task-&lt;t&gt;` (or `--parts` for a re-render); the default is `base+shape-session+task-feature`. The header block carries the launch path, the spec and tests-map paths with their commits, the assembled version, the run log, the prior reports for the same spec, where the plan is written and where the evidence page lives.
8	
9	`kickoff.md` is rendered, never hand-edited. A conduct change is an edit to a part here, with that part's version bumped; the next render picks it up.
10	
11	## Parts
12	
13	- `base.md` — what every orchestrated run shares: the conduct sequence with its three halting gates, the escalation rule, the roles and what each receives, the communication protocol, the budget defaults, the outputs, and the never list.
14	- `shape-session.md`, `shape-workflow.md`, `shape-sessions.md` — what differs by mechanism: how units are dispatched, what counts as progress, and how the run stops. Exactly one is chosen: `shape-session` by default, dispatching subagents from the one orchestrator session; `shape-workflow` when a wave holds more units than the `implementers_concurrent` ceiling, so a workflow script holds the fan-out; `shape-sessions` when a set of units needs its own context window across hours rather than turns.
15	- `task-feature.md`, `task-migration.md`, `task-audit.md`, `task-agent.md` — what differs by kind of work: how the units are cut, what wave 0 (the serial first wave, the one the contracts phase runs) owes, and the risks worth naming. Exactly one is chosen: `task-feature` for new behaviour, `task-migration` for converting an existing form to a new one, `task-audit` for answering questions about a codebase without changing it, `task-agent` for work whose behaviour varies between runs.
16	
17	## Rules for a part
18	
19	- The first line is `&lt;!-- version: N --&gt;` and nothing else. The assembled kickoff version is the `+`-joined list of `part@version`, and it is recorded in the launch's `launch.json`, in the `kickoff_version` field of the launch's `plan.json`, and in the run-log entry.
20	- Agent names appear in backticks under the `## Roles` heading and only there; `fc validate kickoff` refuses a backticked name with no `flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/&lt;name&gt;.md`.
21	- Point, never paste: name paths and commands, and let the orchestrator read the file. A pasted copy of the spec or the roster goes stale the moment its source moves.
22	- Conduct only. A rule the verifier or the critic must obey belongs in the spec, because neither ever sees the kickoff. A rule every session must obey belongs in the project constitution. A rule for one run belongs in the plan.
23	- Short and absolute in the never list, positive everywhere else, and every never has its enforcement named beside it.
24	- Prune on the same pass as you add. A line earns its place only where removing it would cause a mistake and no hook enforces it better than prose can; that is why the version rule above exists, not an approval anyone records.
```

### shape-session.md (version 2)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 2 --&gt;
2	## Shape: session
3	The orchestrator runs in one Claude Code session and dispatches the crew as subagents. Under this shape every parallel wave of the plan fits within the implementers_concurrent ceiling in one turn; the plan validator enforces that.
4	Dispatch: one subagent per unit, worktree-isolated, launched from the prompt `fc worker render` produced for that unit; the units of a parallel wave run as concurrent subagents.
5	Progress: the sequence of returns stored with `fc return` and the events the hooks record in the launch folder's events file, summarised by `fc events summary`; the evidence page is the display, and the session's own scrollback is not evidence.
6	Stopping: the three gates halt the session's turn; the stop gate holds the turn in the contracts and verify phases; on a halt return the orchestrator stops dispatching at once, runs `fc launch escalate halt --detail "…"` and ends the turn.
```

### shape-sessions.md (version 2)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 2 --&gt;
2	## Shape: sessions
3	The run is split across more than one Claude Code session against the same launch: one conducting session that owns the gates, and one further session per stream. A stream is a named set of unit ids drawn from the plan's waves; the conducting session assigns every stream and records the assignment with `fc launch note` before any stream session starts. A further session is opened by starting a new Claude Code session in the same repository with `FLIGHTCREW_LAUNCH=&lt;launch&gt;` set, running `fc launch status` first and reading this launch's kickoff.md and nothing else before it dispatches. The conduct sequence, the gates and every fc command are as the Conduct section above states them.
4	Dispatch: each session dispatches implementers for the units of its own stream only, and only from prompts rendered by `fc worker render` in that session. Before rendering a unit, a session confirms the unit is in its own stream by reading the assignment note with `fc launch status`; two sessions never hold the same unit. The conducting session divides the implementers_concurrent ceiling into a stated share per session when it assigns the streams, and each session holds to its own share.
5	Progress: the shared state is the launch folder — the stored returns, the events file at events.jsonl inside it, and the evidence page — together with the run branch and the unit branches in git. A session that needs to know what another has done reads `fc launch status` and the returns, never the other session's transcript. Only the conducting session stores returns with `fc return worker` and lands units with `fc worker merge` in wave order; stream sessions dispatch and hand their payloads to it.
6	Stopping: only the conducting session records gates and runs `fc launch end &lt;accepted|accepted-with-reservations|abandoned|partial&gt;`. A stream session runs `fc launch status` before each dispatch chunk, dispatches nothing while a gate is pending or an escalation is open, and stops dispatching and ends its turn once the launch status is no longer active. A stream session that meets a halt return, or that reaches one of the launch.json ceilings `fc budget` counts, runs `fc launch escalate &lt;kind&gt; --detail "…"` and ends its turn; the escalation is visible to every session through the launch folder.
```

### shape-workflow.md (version 2)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 2 --&gt;
2	## Shape: workflow
3	The orchestrator hands a wave to a dynamic workflow script — the scripts are kept at flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/ and run from the copies `fc distribute --apply` places in the harness directory's workflows folder — and keeps the session for the gates and the fc commands. This run takes the workflow shape because a wave holds more units than the implementers_concurrent ceiling, so the fan-out needs a script to hold it. The orchestrator session keeps the three gates, every fc command and every stored return; only the dispatch mechanism moves into the script.
4	Dispatch: the scripts are invoked as `/fc-implement`, `/fc-review` and `/fc-explore`, each with a structured args object; flightdeck/manuals/harness/workflows.md carries the args every one takes, the payloads each returns and the command that stores each payload. `/fc-implement` fans the wave's implementers, pilots first and the rest in chunks; `/fc-review` runs the critic, fix and re-verify loop; `/fc-explore` fans the planning questions. Before `/fc-implement`, run `fc worker render &lt;unit&gt;` for every unit of the wave and commit each rendered prompt, because a subagent worktree branches from HEAD; before `/fc-review`, run `fc critic render --pass &lt;n&gt;` and pass that sealed path. The scripts replace only the dispatching in conduct steps 1, 4 and 6; every fc command in those steps stays with the orchestrator, and after a workflow completes the orchestrator persists every payload with `fc return` and lands units with `fc worker merge`.
5	Progress: while a script runs, its phase lines in the `/workflows` view are the display and never evidence; progress is the returns stored with `fc return` and the evidence page once the workflow completes.
6	Stopping: a workflow stops dispatching on the first halt payload and returns it; `/fc-review` returns an escalation payload on a spec conflict and a trigger payload at the critic-passes ceiling with blocking findings open. The orchestrator records each with `fc launch escalate &lt;halt|spec-gap|trigger&gt; --detail "…"` and ends the turn; findings under `unrouted` and answers under `rejected` are placed by the orchestrator before anything is stored. Gates are never inside a script — the script ends and the session halts at the gate.
```

### task-agent.md (version 2)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 2 --&gt;
2	## Task: agent
3	The product is itself agent-shaped: prompts, agent definitions, tools or workflows whose output varies between runs. The spec tags every behaviour with the class its check belongs to, and the class is carried on the check entry in the pinned tests map; a unit inherits it through the check ids it names. Cut one unit per tagged behaviour, each unit holding checks of a single class, so a varying unit's evidence is never mixed with a deterministic one's. Wave 0 is the single `kind: contracts` unit and delivers the harness the varying classes are measured through — the generator entry point, the fixed sample and the rubric file — with its contract checks green before any statistical or judged unit starts.
4	Read the pinned tests map at step 1 and confirm each check of these classes carries what its class owes; a check that does not is a spec silence and goes to `fc launch escalate spec-gap --detail "…"` before the plan is written. The four classes and what each owes: `[deterministic]` — an exact check, run once, pass or fail. `[property]` — an invariant that must hold over every generated case, with the generator and its seed pinned in the check. `[statistical]` — a rate over a fixed sample, with the sample, the threshold and the tolerance stated before the run and the observed rate recorded in the evidence. `[judged]` — a rubric a human applies, reading it at gate 3 beside the ledger and the unverified lines, with the verdict appended by `fc events append &lt;json&gt; --stated`.
5	Build the deterministic units first: they hold the shape the varying ones are measured inside. A statistical or judged behaviour that reaches plan time with no threshold or rubric written down is not the plan's to record: run `fc launch escalate spec-gap --detail "…"` naming the behaviour, because only a human can enter it in the tests map's unverified list with a reason. The report's unverified line then carries it to gate 3.
6	Risks worth naming: a threshold chosen after seeing the result, a sample too small to separate the classes, and a judged behaviour whose rubric drifts between passes.
```

### task-audit.md (version 2)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 2 --&gt;
2	## Task: audit
3	The deliverable is findings and their evidence. Cut the units by question, one question per unit, and give each question unit the check id from the pinned tests map that reproduces what it claims: an audit finding without a reproduction is an opinion. A question whose reproduction check the pinned map does not carry is a spec gap, raised with `fc launch escalate spec-gap --detail "…"` before gate 1; no check is written during the run.
4	Wave 0 is the single `kind: contracts` unit and builds the harness the audit reads through — the script, the fixture or the query every later unit reuses — at `flightdeck/launch/&lt;launch&gt;/audit/harness/`, so that every finding is produced the same way; its own check is the harness's. Later units answer one question each and write their finding and its reproduction output to `flightdeck/launch/&lt;launch&gt;/audit/findings/&lt;unit&gt;/`, the path their plan entry grants and no fc command rewrites.
5	Read widely and write narrowly: a question unit's paths are its findings directory alone, and wave 0's are the harness directory. An allowed-path set arriving from the pinned tests map that is wider than the harness and the launch folder is an escalation, not something the plan narrows silently. A unit given no write path outside those returns a halt rather than applying a remedy it found; record the finding and dispatch no fix. Remedies belong to a later run against a spec written from these findings.
6	Risks worth naming: a question that cannot be answered from the repository, a finding that only reproduces on one machine, and a sweep that quietly becomes a fix.
```

### task-feature.md (version 2)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 2 --&gt;
2	## Task: feature
3	Cut the work vertically: each feature unit delivers one testable behaviour end to end and names the checks that prove it. Wave 0 holds the single `kind: contracts` unit, which fixes the interfaces the spec names and passes their contract checks before any feature unit starts. The last wave holds one unit of `kind: proof`, which integrates and whose checks include the acceptance check `T1` from the pinned tests map.
4	A unit that cannot name a check is not a unit: fold it into one that can. A behaviour the pinned tests map does not check is a spec gap, raised with `fc launch escalate spec-gap --detail "…"` before the plan is written; the pinned map changes only by a re-pin, never by the orchestrator. Prefer more small units over fewer wide ones — one behaviour per unit, a diff a reviewer reads in one pass — and never let two units in the same parallel wave list overlapping paths. A file may be revisited by a unit in a later wave, which merges after it; where an overlap inside one wave cannot be avoided, carry it into the plan's risks.
5	Risks worth naming: two units editing one file, an interface that wave 0 guessed, and a behaviour whose check runs so slowly that an implementer cannot edit and re-run it inside its turn budget.
```

### task-migration.md (version 2)
```
1	&lt;!-- version: 2 --&gt;
2	## Task: migration
3	The work is a sweep: the same mechanical change across many call sites, with behaviour held constant. Cut the units by area of the tree, and size each one so it converts at most one area and its diff stays inside the unit's turn budget.
4	Wave 0 is the single `kind: contracts` unit: it lands the new form and the compatibility surface, and its contract check proves the old and the new forms agree. Every later unit is `kind: feature`, converts one area, and re-runs the behavioural check ids that covered that area before the conversion. The last wave holds one `kind: proof` unit that deletes the compatibility surface and carries the acceptance check, which proves the old form is gone; that check is expected to flip there and nowhere earlier.
5	Hold the line that a migration changes no behaviour: a unit that finds a defect on the way converts the call site as it stands and names the defect in its return notes, and the orchestrator records it with `fc launch note`. Fixing it inside the sweep makes it indistinguishable from the mechanical change, so neither the diff nor the critic can isolate it.
6	Risks worth naming: a call site the sweep cannot reach, a check that passes because both forms are present, and a rename that collides with an unconverted area.
```

---

## Analysis Per Part

**base.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/base.md:1-28): Seven-step conduct sequence (explore → plan → contracts → waves → verify → review → report) with three halting gates, escalation protocol for spec gaps, wrong checks, triggers, blocked actions, and budget exceeded; roles named are `explorer`, `planner`, `implementer`, `verifier`, `critic`; ceilings: implementers_concurrent, agents, turns per agent, gate iterations, stop blocks, critic passes, minutes, tokens.

**README.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/README.md:1-25): Assembly mechanism for kickoff.md via version-tracked parts; validates agent names exist in flightdeck/flightcrew/crew/; dispatch is rendering by fc; ceiling: implementers_concurrent determines choice of shape.

**shape-session.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/shape-session.md:1-6): Single Claude Code session dispatching concurrent subagents per unit; progress tracked via fc return events and evidence page; gates halt the session's turn; ceiling: implementers_concurrent must fit one parallel wave in one turn.

**shape-sessions.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/shape-sessions.md:1-6): Multi-session orchestration with one conducting session owning gates and divisions; stream sessions confirm unit assignment, dispatch, and hand payloads; ceiling: implementers_concurrent divided among sessions.

**shape-workflow.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/shape-workflow.md:1-7): Workflow script (/fc-implement, /fc-explore, /fc-review) dispatches wave when it exceeds implementers_concurrent ceiling; orchestrator retains gates and fc commands; obligation to pre-render all unit prompts and commit before /fc-implement; ceiling trigger: wave exceeds implementers_concurrent.

**task-agent.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/task-agent.md:1-6): Agent-shaped work with four check classes (deterministic, property, statistical, judged) assigned to units; wave 0 contracts unit delivers harness (generator, sample, rubric); obligation to build deterministic units first, escalate if statistical/judged lacks threshold/rubric before plan time.

**task-audit.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/task-audit.md:1-6): Questions cut into units per pinned check ids; wave 0 contracts unit builds reusable harness at audit/harness/; findings go to audit/findings/&lt;unit&gt;/; obligation "read widely, write narrowly"; units with no write paths halt rather than apply remedies.

**task-feature.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/task-feature.md:1-5): Vertical cut delivering one testable behaviour per unit; wave 0 contracts unit fixes interfaces; last wave proof unit includes acceptance check T1; obligations: no overlapping paths within one wave, one behaviour per unit, no hand-editable checks.

**task-migration.md** (flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/kickoff/task-migration.md:1-6): Mechanical sweep across tree areas; wave 0 contracts lands new form and compatibility surface; later feature units re-run prior behavioural checks; last wave proof unit deletes compatibility and carries acceptance check that flips there; obligation to hold the line: no hidden changes.

[ASSISTANT 💬]
Here's the library against the suite idea, part by part. This is a proposal to react to, nothing written.

**base.md** — its seven conduct steps are the suite. Each step that dispatches agents becomes a workflow; what sits between them is you reading. Its other sections dissolve into things the runtime and files already do: escalation → a workflow's typed result (`halt`, `spec-gap`, `stalled`) that ends it; roles and what each receives → the agent files and dispatch templates; communication → return-capture and event-log; budgets → runtime caps and `maxTurns`; never-list → the guards and sandbox. Nothing in base survives as prose.

**The suite I'd propose from those steps:**
- `fc-plan` — explorers, then planner; ends with `plan.json` for you to read.
- `fc-contracts` — interface-builder on wave 0, its checks; ends with the seams for you to read.
- `fc-waves` — implementers by wave, scouts first, worker gate, stronger retry on stall, adversary per unit, merge; ends with every unit merged, red or unmerged.
- `fc-verify` — `flight check` and the verifier's refutation; ends green or naming what failed.
- `fc-review` — critic passes, fix dispatches, re-verify; ends with no open blocking finding or the pass cap.
- `fc-report` — scribe report and log stub, worktrees removed, PR opened, you informed.
You said no human between waves and review; keeping them as separate scripts still lets the orchestrator run them back to back on one instruction, and lets you rerun one alone.

**shape-session / shape-workflow / shape-sessions** — these chose a dispatch mechanism. Under the suite there is one mechanism, the runtime, which caps concurrency itself. `shape-session` and `shape-workflow` collapse into it. `shape-sessions` (several Claude Code sessions sharing one run) is the one that doesn't — it's a different way of driving the same suite from more than one seat.

**task-feature / migration / audit / agent** — these are the ones worth keeping. They don't change the mechanism; they change how the planner cuts units, what wave 0 owes, what the waves may write, and the risks. Two ways to carry them: as a `task` argument in the kickoff that selects a variant of the planner's dispatch template and the waves' rules; or as separate plan workflows (`fc-plan-feature`, `fc-plan-audit`, …).

Three questions:
1. Is that six-workflow suite the right membership, or would you merge or add any?
2. Task kinds: one `task` argument selecting the planner template variant, or a plan workflow per kind?
3. `shape-sessions`: drop it for this version, or keep multi-session driving in scope?

[ASSISTANT 💬]
Understood — and the nesting fact is in the docs and your explainer both (one level, `workflow(name, args)`), so `fc-build` calling `fc-verify` and `fc-review` is a real shape.

Consolidated, here is exactly what I'd write for the unsettled problems. One "go" and I edit; corrections first if any:

**P4 — liftoff and the suite**
- `I14 liftoff.json`: `flightdeck/flightcrew/templates/liftoff.template.json` copied by `flight launch run` to `runs/run-<n>/liftoff.json`, schema'd, frozen before the first workflow. Shape: `{ launch, run, spec: {version, commit}, tests_map: {version, commit}, task: feature|migration|audit|agent, sequence: [ { workflow: <name>, args: {…} } ], sessions?: [ { name, units: [ids] } ], models: {…}, review: {…}, previous_entry, notes }`. The sequence names the workflows to run in order and the args each gets; `sessions` carries the multi-session shape (streams of units per seat); `notes` is free text for anything else the run needs said.
- Workflows under `flightdeck/flightcrew/workflows/`, installed to `.claude/workflows/`: `fc-plan-feature`, `fc-plan-migration`, `fc-plan-audit`, `fc-plan-agent` (explorers then planner, each with that kind's planner template), `fc-contracts`, `fc-build` (waves; calls `fc-verify` then `fc-review` unless args say otherwise), `fc-verify`, `fc-review`, `fc-report` (scribe report and log stub, worktrees removed, PR opened, you informed). Every one also runs alone.
- The orchestrator invokes the sequence one entry per instruction from you and reports each result. The markdown kickoff library is retired; its task parts become the four planner templates.

**P8 — stall**: worker gate counts its blocks, writes a `stalled` event at the cap and lets the worker return; `fc-build` dispatches the strong-worker once; a second stall ends the workflow naming the unit and the failing check. No halt file.

**P9 — ending**: `fc-report` does the system's part; merge, reject, branch deletion, diagnosis and retry are yours; no `fc-end`, no outcome the system reads.

**P1/P2**: `launch.json` index (spec and map versions, current run, per-run branch and commits), `.controlcenter` written by the launch commands and inherited by worktrees, `.gitignore` line added, no `run.json`, no gates object.

Go?

[ASSISTANT 💬]
Got it — a liftoff is a recipe, not a per-run file: a library under `flightdeck/flightcrew/liftoffs/<name>.json`, each naming the workflow sequence, the overrides and any other settings; you paste one into the orchestrator session and it runs the lot. Tooling is a failure axis, so a run that needs different tooling gets a different liftoff, not a different workflow. The kickoff library's job, in JSON.

Corrected proposal for P4:

- `I14 liftoff`: `flightdeck/flightcrew/liftoffs/<name>.json`, schema'd: `{ name, task: feature|migration|audit|agent, sequence: [ { workflow, args } ], sessions?: [ { name, units } ], models, review, settings: {…}, notes }`. The library ships with a few (names yours to give). The log entry's kickoff line names the liftoff at its commit.
- The orchestrator, handed a liftoff by paste, writes it to `runs/run-<n>/liftoff.json` as the run's record of what it was given, then runs the sequence — one entry per your instruction, reporting each result. Say if you'd rather it not write the copy.
- The nine workflows as listed (four `fc-plan-<kind>`, `fc-contracts`, `fc-build` calling `fc-verify` and `fc-review`, those two standalone too, `fc-report`), each taking its args from the liftoff entry.
- The markdown kickoff library retires; its task parts become the four planner templates.

P8, P9, P1/P2 as in my last message. Go?
